inset-0 mix-blend-overlay rounded-lg z-10"></div></div></div></div><div class="lg:max-w-7xl mx-auto relative mt-12 lg:grid lg:grid-cols-4 lg:gap-8 items-start"><div class="flex justify-center lg:col-span-3"><div class="prose lg:prose-lg prose-invert w-full article-content"><div><h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="introduction" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Introduction</h2>
<p>Elastic Security Labs has been tracking a financially motivated operation, designated REF1695, that has been active since at least late 2023. The operator deploys a combination of RATs, cryptominers, and custom XMRig loaders through fake installer packages. Across all observed campaigns, the infection chains share a consistent packing technique, overlapping C2 infrastructure, and common social engineering patterns, linking them to a single operator.</p>
<p>Beyond cryptomining, the threat actor monetizes infections through CPA (Cost Per Action) fraud, directing victims to content locker pages under the guise of software registration. In this report, we trace the operation&#x27;s evolution across multiple campaign builds, analyze the C2 communication protocols, document a previously unreported .NET implant (CNB Bot), and track the operator&#x27;s financial returns via public Monero mining pool dashboards.</p>
<h3 class="font-bold leading-tight text-xl md:text-3xl relative"><span id="key-takeaways" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Key takeaways</h3>
<ul>
<li>Financially motivated campaigns have been active since late 2023, deploying various RATs and cryptominers through fake installer packages.</li>
<li>Operator monetizes infections through both cryptomining and CPABuild fraud.</li>
<li>Stages use a consistent Themida/WinLicense + .NET Reactor packing combination</li>
<li>CNB Bot is a previously undocumented .NET implant with RSA-2048 signed task authentication</li>
<li>A custom XMRig loader evades detection by killing the miner whenever analysis tools are running and deploys WinRing0x64.sys</li>
<li>Over 27.88 XMR paid out across four tracked wallets, with active workers at the time of writing</li>
<li>We leveraged a Claude-driven agentic pipeline to automate the extraction of payload stages and implant configurations</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="campaign-1-cnb-bot" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Campaign 1 (CNB Bot)</h2>
<p>The most recent campaign involves dropping CNB Bot, using an ISO file as the infection vector. The ISO image contains 2 files: a single-stage .NET Reactor-protected loader further packed with Themida/WinLicense 3.x, and a ReadMe.txt. Associated ISO samples:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">460203070b5a928390b126fcd52c15ed3a668b77536faa6f0a0282cf1c157162</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">b8b7aecce2a4d00f209b1e4d30128ba6ef0f83bbdc05127f6f8ba97e7d6df291</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">9977b9185472c7d4be22c20f93bc401dd74bb47223957015a3261994d54c59fc</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">9fa23382820b1e781f3e05e9452176a72529395643f09080777fab7b9c6b1f5c</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">27db41f654b53e41a4e1621a83f2478fa46b1bbffc1923e5070440a7d410b8d3</code></li>
</ul>
<p>The ReadMe.txt serves as a social engineering lure, framing the unsigned binary as the product of a small non-profit team that cannot afford EV code-signing, then provides explicit instructions to bypass SmartScreen via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">&quot;More Info&quot; → &quot;Run Anyway.&quot;</code>.</p>
<p></p>
<p>Using the open-source Themida/Winlicense unpacker project, <a href="https://github.com/ergrelet/unlicense">Unlicense</a>, we automatically extracted the .NET Reactor-protected loader and then passed it through <a href="https://github.com/SychicBoy/NETReactorSlayer">NETReactorSlayer</a> for deobfuscation. The majority of campaigns were observed to use this combination of protection in both the initial and subsequent stages.</p>
<p>The loader first invokes PowerShell with <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">-WindowStyle Hidden</code>, to register broad Microsoft Defender exclusions via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Add-MpPreference -ExclusionPath</code> and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Add-MpPreference -ExclusionProcess</code>, covering the loader itself, staging directories (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%</code>, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%LocalAppData%</code>, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%AppData%</code>) and a set of LOLBin process names the malware later utilizes.</p>
<p></p>
<p>It then extracts an embedded .NET assembly resource and writes it to disk at <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%\MLPCInstallHelper.exe</code> (filename varies by build), then executes it via PowerShell. This embedded resource is a .NET Reactor-protected CNB Bot instance, discussed in detail in the <strong>Code Analysis - CNB Bot</strong> section below.</p>
<p>Since no legitimate software is installed at any point, the loader presents a fake error dialog to the user, attributing the installation failure to unmet system requirements.</p>
<p></p>
<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="campaign-2-purerat" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Campaign 2 (PureRAT)</h2>
<p>Pivoting on the ReadMe.txt lure content, we discovered a campaign dropping PureRAT v3.0.1. This campaign uses a very similar initial-stage loader as campaign 1 and introduces a second-stage loader.</p>
<p>Example ISO samples employing this chain:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">7bb0e91558244bcc79b6d7a4fe9d9882f11d3a99b70e1527aac979e27165f1d7</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">c6c4a9725653b585a9d65fc90698d4610579b289bcfb2539f7a5f7e64e69f2e4</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">a3f84aa1d15fd33506157c61368fd602d0b81f69aff6c69249bf833d217308bb</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">82c03866670b70047209c39153615512f7253f125a252fe3dcd828c6598fdf86</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">542d2267b40c160b693646bc852df34cc508281c4f6ed2693b98147dae293678</code></li>
</ul>
<p>We will be using the first sample from this list as an example for our analysis.</p>
<p>The initial-stage loader applies Microsoft Defender exclusions to the same directory set (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%</code>, loader path, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%LocalAppData%</code>, …), but process exclusions are limited to the loader executable only. The Stage 2 payload is extracted from the embedded resource to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%\&lt;...&gt;InstallHelper.exe</code> and launched via hidden PowerShell <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Start-Process</code>. Stage 2 is protected with the same Themida + .NET Reactor packing technique.</p>
<p>Stage 2 registers only process-level Microsoft Defender exclusions.</p>
<p></p>
<p>The loader then extracts four embedded resources into the install directory at <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%SystemDrive%\Users\%UserName%\AppData\Local\SVCData\Config</code>, dropping 3 unused, benign DLLs and a malicious <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">svchost.exe</code> binary, which is the 3rd stage. Stage 3 is launched through PowerShell, and a scheduled task named <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">SVCConfig</code> is registered via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">schtasks.exe</code> with an <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">ONLOGON</code> trigger and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">HIGHEST</code> privilege.</p>
<p></p>
<p>Following payload launch, Stage 2 writes a temporary .bat file to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%</code> with a polling loop that forcefully deletes the installer binary until successful, then deletes the batch file itself.</p>
<p></p>
<p>Stage 3 is a Themida + .NET Reactor-protected, in-memory PE loader, which is also the beginning of the PureRAT component. The encrypted next-stage module is stored as a .NET resource and decrypted via Triple DES (3DES) in CBC mode using an embedded key and IV. The decrypted output is a GZip-compressed PE: the first 4 bytes encode the decompressed size as a little-endian integer, followed by the GZip stream.</p>
<p></p>
<p>The PureRAT v3.0.1 configuration is decoded by base64-decoding an embedded string and deserializing the result as a Protobuf message:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">23-01-26</code> (build / campaign date)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">windirautoupdates[.]top</code> (C2 #1)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">winautordr.itemdb[.]com</code> (C2 #2)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">winautordr.ydns[.]eu</code> (C2 #3)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">winautordr.kozow[.]com</code>  (C2 #4)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Aesthetics135</code> (mutex and C2 comms key)</li>
</ul>
<p></p>
<p>The C2 communication protocol uses key derivation function - <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">PBKDF2-SHA1(&quot;Aesthetics135&quot;, embedded_salt=010217EA2530863FF804, iter=5000)</code> to derive 96 bytes, split into an AES-256-CBC key and an HMAC-SHA256 key. Incoming messages are authenticated by verifying the HMAC over <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">[IV | ciphertext]</code> stored in the first 32 bytes; the IV is then read from byte offset (32- 48) and used to decrypt the remaining ciphertext, yielding a <a href="https://protobuf.dev/">Protobuf</a>-encoded command message.</p>
<p>By decrypting traffic captured in VirusTotal sandboxes, we observed that the C2 server at <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">windirautoupdates[.]top</code> was automatically issuing a download-and-execute task directing the implant to fetch an XMR mining payload from <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://github[.]com/lebnabar198/Hgh5gM99fe3dG/raw/refs/heads/main/MnrsInstllr_240126[.]exe</code>.</p>
<p></p>
<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="campaign-3-purerat-pureminer-xmrig-loader" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Campaign 3 (PureRAT, PureMiner, XMRig loader)</h2>
<p>The third campaign variant shares the same initial-stage loader design as Campaigns 1 and 2. Its Stage 2 resembles Campaign 2 but differs by dropping multiple embedded payloads from the resource section, including PureRAT, a custom XMRig loader, and PureMiner.</p>
<p>Example ISO sample:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">f84b00fc75f183c571c8f49fcc1d7e0241f538025db0f2daa4e2c5b9a6739049</code>.</li>
</ul>
<p></p>
<p>To keep the machine awake and maximize mining uptime, the loader disables sleep and hibernation via Windows power management commands:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">powercfg /change standby-timeout-ac 0</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">powercfg /change standby-timeout-dc 0</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">powercfg /change hibernate-timeout-ac 0</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">powercfg /change hibernate-timeout-dc 0</code></li>
</ul>
<p>The PureRAT configuration matches Campaign 2, differing only in the build/campaign ID: <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">25-11-25</code>.</p>
<p>The PE loader component of PureMiner is similar to PureRAT, and the decrypted module is also obfuscated via .NET Reactor. Since the configuration is Protobuf-serialized, hooking <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">ProtoBuf.Serializer::Deserialize</code> allows inspection of the configuration data:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">25-11-25</code> (build / campaign date)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.hopto[.]org</code> (C2 #1)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.itemdb[.]com</code> (C2 #2)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.ydns[.]eu</code> (C2 #3)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.kozow[.]com</code> (C2 #4)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">4c271ad41ea2f6a44ce8d0</code> (mutex and C2 comms key)</li>
</ul>
<p></p>
<p>Additional behavioral indicators include the dynamic loading of AMD Display Library binaries (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">atiadlxx.dll</code>/<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">atiadlxy.dll</code>) and the NVIDIA API library (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">nvapi64.dll</code>), consistent with GPU hardware profiling techniques employed by PureMiner.</p>
<p></p>
<h3 class="font-bold leading-tight text-xl md:text-3xl relative"><span id="custom-net-based-loader-for-xmrig" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Custom .NET-Based Loader for XMRig</h3>
<p>The following findings cover the custom XMRig loader deployed during this campaign. Analyzed samples:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">0176ffaf278b9281aa207c59b858c8c0b6e38fdb13141f7ed391c9f8b2dc7630</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">9409f9c398645ddac096e3331d2782705b62e388a8ecb1c4e9d527616f0c6a9e</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">f84b00fc75f183c571c8f49fcc1d7e0241f538025db0f2daa4e2c5b9a6739049</code></li>
</ul>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="the-entry-point-and-setup" class="absolute -top-32"></span>The Entry Point and Setup</h4>
<p>Execution begins in the <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Start()</code> method. The loader first calls <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">FetchRemoteConfig()</code>, which reaches out to a hardcoded URL (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://autoupdatewinsystem[.]top/MyMNRconfigs/0226.txt</code>). The response is AES-encrypted JSON, which the loader decrypts using a hardcoded key (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">AsyncPrivateInputx64</code>) and parses to extract the pool, wallet, and mining arguments. If the remote server is unreachable or decryption fails, it falls back to a hardcoded <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">ztbpVbABSx1jDIKnWGbx1d_0</code> configuration to ensure mining can still occur.</p>
<p></p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="resource-extraction" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Resource Extraction</h4>
<p>Simultaneously, an asynchronous task triggers <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">ExtractResources()</code>. The loader checks the <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%</code> directory for two files: <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">procsrv.exe</code> (the renamed XMRig payload) and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">WinRing0x64.sys</code> (a driver used by XMRig for direct hardware access). If either is absent, the loader unpacks them from its own assembly manifest.</p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="evasion-loop" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Evasion Loop</h4>
<p>After a 3-second sleep, the loader calls <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">StartEvasionTimer()</code>, initializing a timer that ticks every 1,000 milliseconds. On each tick, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">IsAnalysisToolRunning()</code> compares all running process names against a hardcoded list of 35 security and monitoring tools (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Taskmgr</code>, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">ProcessHacker</code>, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Wireshark</code>, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Procmon</code>, etc.).</p>
<p></p>
<p>If any analysis tool is detected, the loader immediately calls <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">KillMinerProcess()</code>, terminating <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">procsrv.exe</code>, effectively dropping the CPU usage back to normal.</p>
<p>If no analysis tool is detected, the loader calls <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">CheckAndRunMiner()</code>. If the miner is not currently running, it reconstructs the command-line arguments (using the remote or fallback config) and quietly launches the miner as a hidden background process via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">LaunchMiner()</code>.</p>
<p>This creates a &quot;hide and seek&quot; scenario for the victim. Whenever they try to investigate why their PC is slow, the malware shuts down the miner.</p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="winring0x64sys-and-ring-0-access" class="absolute -top-32"></span>WinRing0x64.sys and Ring 0 Access</h4>
<p>The loader also drops and loads <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">WinRing0x64.sys</code>, a legitimate open-source driver frequently abused by cryptominers. The driver provides direct Ring 0 (kernel-level) hardware access, which XMRig uses to apply its Model Specific Register (MSR) modification, reconfiguring CPU prefetcher and L3 cache behavior to significantly boost RandomX (Monero) hash rates.</p>
<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="campaign-4---umnr_-silentcryptominer" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Campaign 4 - Umnr_ (SilentCryptoMiner)</h2>
<p>From the <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">autoupdatewinsystem[.]top</code> domain, we identified another GitHub account <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://github[.]com/ugurlutaha6116</code> hosting another loader variant whose executable name is prefixed with <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Umnr_</code>. This loader is a Themida-packed SilentCryptoMiner loader that installs persistently on the victim machine, injects a watchdog payload into <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">conhost.exe</code>, and a miner payload into <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">explorer.exe</code>, mining ETH or XMR depending on the build configuration.</p>
<p>SilentCryptoMiner is a closed-source Win32 64-bit malware released for free on <a href="https://github.com/Unam-Sanctam/SilentCryptoMiner">GitHub</a>. The samples we analyzed are older versions than the latest <a href="https://github.com/Unam-Sanctam/SilentCryptoMiner/releases">release</a>:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">1f7441d72eff2e9403be1d9ce0bb07792793b2cb963f2601ecfdf8c91cd9af73</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">468441d32f62520020d57ff1f24bb08af1bc10e9b4d4da1b937450f44e80a9be</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">4e6b8fdd819293ca3fe8f8add6937bf6531a936955d9ac974a6b231823c7330e</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">6492e50e79b979254314988228a513d5acbdaa950346414955dc052ae77d2988</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">ce90cb3a9bfb8a276cb50462be932e063ed408af8c5591dd2c50f1c6d18c394c</code></li>
</ul>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="direct-syscalls" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Direct Syscalls</h4>
<p>To evade detection, SilentCryptoMiner uses direct syscalls instead of <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NTDLL</code> functions. To do this, it parses <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NTDLL</code> exports to locate the target function by a hash of its name, extracts the syscall number, and manually executes the syscall instruction sequence.</p>
<p></p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="disable-sleep-and-hibernate" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Disable Sleep and Hibernate</h4>
<p>To ensure it can use the host machine for as long as possible, SilentCryptoMiner disables Windows sleep and hibernation by executing a shell command.</p>
<p></p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="install-persistence" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Install Persistence</h4>
<p>After copying itself to its installation folder (in this case, configured to masquerade as legitimate software named “<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Appdata/Local/OptimizeMS/optims.exe</code>”), SilentCryptoMiner proceeds to establish persistence. If the process is running with administrator privileges, it creates a scheduled task configured via an XML file.</p>
<p></p>
<p>The XML file is dropped onto the disk in the <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">AppData/Local/Temp</code> folder and contains the task configuration. One interesting setting is <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">AllowHardTerminate = False</code>, which prevents the task from being forcibly terminated via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">schtasks</code>.</p>
<p></p>
<p>If the process lacks administrator rights, it instead adds a <strong>Run</strong> key to the registry.</p>
<p></p>
<p>After initial installation, the process terminates. On subsequent execution by the persistence mechanism, it verifies that it is running from its installation directory before proceeding to the process injection phase.</p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="inject-watchdog-and-miner-payloads" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Inject watchdog and miner payloads</h4>
<p>In the samples we analyzed, the builds contain four payloads:</p>
<ul>
<li>A <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Winring0.sys</code> driver</li>
<li>A watchdog process</li>
<li>A Monero miner</li>
<li>An Ethereum miner</li>
</ul>
<p>We know that the malware can contain multiple miners; however, in our tests, we only observed the Monero miner injected into a process. In the code, only one of the two miners is injected, which we assume depends on the configuration.</p>
<p>SilentCryptoMiner initiates injection by creating a new suspended process with a spoofed parent process. It obtains a handle to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">explorer.exe</code> using <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtQuerySystemInformation</code> and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtOpenProcess</code>, then configures a <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">PS_ATTRIBUTE_LIST</code> structure with the handle for parent spoofing and passes it to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtCreateUserProcess</code>.</p>
<p>The payload is written to disk via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtCreateFile</code> and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtWriteFile</code>, then mapped into the target process&#x27;s memory space through <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtCreateSection</code> and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtMapViewOfSection</code>. Execution flow is hijacked by modifying the suspended process&#x27;s entry point (in the <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">RCX</code> register) to point to the payload&#x27;s image base using <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtGetContextThread</code> and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtSetContextThread</code>. The process&#x27;s PEB (in <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">RDX</code> register) image base is also set to the payload&#x27;s address using <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtWriteVirtualMemory</code>. Finally, the process is resumed with <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">NtResumeThread</code>.</p>
<p></p>
<p>The payload data is decrypted from a hardcoded blob in the binary using a simple XOR cipher with a hardcoded key. After injection, the blob is re-encrypted in memory to reduce forensic traces.</p>
<p></p>
<p>In the analyzed samples, SilentCryptoMiner utilizes two distinct processes for payload injection: the watchdog component is injected into <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">conhost.exe</code>, while the miner payload targets <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">explorer.exe</code>. The <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">WinRing0.sys</code> driver is also written to disk, then loaded and used by the miner. This is likely to optimize the CPU for mining operations.</p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="watchdog-and-miner-processes" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Watchdog and Miner Processes</h4>
<p>The watchdog is responsible for monitoring the loader file in its persistence folder: it rewrites the file to disk if it is deleted and reinstalls the persistence mechanism if the scheduled task or registry key is deleted.</p>
<p>The miner downloads its configuration from <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">(/UWP1)?/*CPU.txt</code> endpoints and communicates with its C2 via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">[UWP1|UnamWebPanel7]/api/endpoint.php</code> API, depending on the version.</p>
<p>Based on the documentation and memory strings, we know that the miner includes supplementary protection measures: Like the .NET miner detailed previously, it halts mining operations when it detects specific blocklisted processes. These processes encompass a variety of tools, including those used for process monitoring, network monitoring, antivirus protection, and reverse engineering.</p>
<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="code-analysis---cnb-bot" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Code analysis - CNB Bot</h2>
<p>CNB Bot is a .NET implant with integrated loader capabilities. It implements a command-polling loop against its configured C2 servers, and supports 3 operator commands:</p>
<ul>
<li>download-and-execute arbitrary payloads</li>
<li>self-update</li>
<li>uninstall/cleanup</li>
</ul>
<p>On Jan 31, 2026, malware researcher <a href="https://x.com/ViriBack/status/2017388775978967074">@ViriBack</a> discovered a related C2 panel that was exposed at <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://win64autoupdates[.]top/CNB/l0g1n234[.]php</code>, which has since been taken offline.</p>
<p></p>
<h3 class="font-bold leading-tight text-xl md:text-3xl relative"><span id="configuration" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Configuration</h3>
<p>Some configuration values for CNB Bot are not encrypted, such as the bot version (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">1.1.6.</code>), campaign date (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">03_26</code>), and the scheduled task name for persistence (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">HostDataPlugin</code>).</p>
<p></p>
<p>Sensitive strings (C2 URLs, mutex name, auth token, comms key) are stored AES-256-CBC encrypted with a hardcoded 32-byte key, which differs across campaign batches.</p>
<p>Strings can be decrypted through the following formula:</p>
<pre><code>x = base64.decode(data)
decrypted = AES256CBC(key=hard_coded_key, iv=x[0:16]).decrypt(x[16:])</code></pre>
<p>Extracted configuration:</p>
<div class="table-container"><table style="width:100%;table-layout:fixed;word-wrap:break-word"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Field</th><th style="text-align:left">Value</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left">Mutex Name</td><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">MTXCNBV11000ERCXSWOLZNBVRGH</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">C2 URL</td><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://tommysbakescodes[.]ws/CNB/gate.php</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">C2 URL style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">AnCnDai@4zDsxP!a3E</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Scheduled Task</td><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%APPDATA%\HostData\</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Marker File</td><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%APPDATA%\HostData\install.dat</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Executable</td><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">sysdata.exe</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Group / Campaign</td><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">03_26</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Bot Version</td><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">1.1.6.</code></td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<h3 class="font-bold leading-tight text-xl md:text-3xl relative"><span id="execution-flow" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Execution Flow</h3>
<p>At startup, CNB Bot uses five different methods to check for VM detection:</p>
<div class="table-container"><table style="width:100%;table-layout:fixed;word-wrap:break-word"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Check</th><th style="text-align:left">Technique</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left">WMI ComputerSystem</td><td style="text-align:left">Manufacturer/Model: &quot;vmware&quot;, &quot;virtualbox&quot;, &quot;vbox&quot;, &quot;qemu&quot;, &quot;xen&quot;, &quot;parallels&quot;, &quot;innotek&quot;, &quot;microsoft corporation&quot; (manufacturer) + &quot;virtual machine&quot; (model)</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">WMI BIOS</td><td style="text-align:left">Version/Serial: &quot;vmware&quot;, &quot;virtualbox&quot;, &quot;vbox&quot;, &quot;qemu&quot;, &quot;bochs&quot;, &quot;seabios&quot;</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Process list</td><td style="text-align:left">&quot;vmtoolsd&quot;, &quot;vmwaretray&quot;, &quot;vmwareuser&quot;, &quot;vboxservice&quot;, &quot;vboxtray&quot;, &quot;xenservice&quot;</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">Registry</td><td style="text-align:left">VMware Tools / VirtualBox Guest Additions keys: &quot;SOFTWARE\VMware, Inc.\VMware Tools&quot;, &quot;SOFTWARE\Oracle\VirtualBox Guest Additions&quot;, &quot;SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\VBoxGuest&quot;, &quot;SYSTEM\CurrentControlSet\Services\VBoxSF&quot;</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left">MAC Address</td><td style="text-align:left">&quot;00:0C:29&quot;, &quot;00:50:56&quot;, &quot;00:05:69&quot;, &quot;08:00:27&quot;, &quot;0A:00:27&quot;, &quot;00:16:3E&quot;, &quot;00:1C:14&quot;</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>Each check returns zero or one and is summed against a threshold. When the detection threshold is reached, the first process instance acquires a named mutex and enters an infinite sleep <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">(Thread.Sleep(int.MaxValue))</code>, appearing hung rather than terminating cleanly. Any subsequent instance finding the mutex already held exits immediately.</p>
<p>Otherwise, on first execution, the implant checks for <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%APPDATA%\HostData\install.dat</code>. If absent, it performs the initial installation:</p>
<ul>
<li>Generates a random 5-character alphabetic subdirectory name under <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%APPDATA%\HostData\</code></li>
<li>Copies itself to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%APPDATA%\HostData\&lt;random&gt;\sysdata.exe</code></li>
<li>Writes the installed path to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">install.dat</code></li>
<li>Extracts benign dependencies <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">DiagSvc.dll</code> and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">sdrsvc.dll</code> into the same directory</li>
<li>Writes a VBScript wrapper <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">sysdata.vbs</code> alongside the binary: <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">CreateObject(&quot;WScript.Shell&quot;).Run &quot;&quot;&quot;&lt;installed_path&gt;&quot;&quot;&quot;, 0, False</code></li>
<li>Creates a scheduled task named <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">HostDataProcess</code> via schtasks.exe, configured to run <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wscript.exe //nologo sysdata.vbs</code> every 10 minutes at <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">HIGHEST</code> privilege</li>
<li>Launches the installed copy as a hidden process with <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%</code> as the working directory</li>
<li>Self-deletes the original copy via a self-deleting BAT script (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">timeout /t 3</code>, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">loop-del</code>)</li>
</ul>
<p>On subsequent runs, when <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">install.dat</code> exists, and the running path matches its contents, the implant proceeds to active operation:</p>
<ul>
<li>Sets the current working directory to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%</code></li>
<li>Repairs persistence: checks if <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">sysdata.vbs</code> exists (recreates if absent) and verifies the scheduled task is configured with <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wscript.exe</code>, re-registering it if necessary</li>
<li>Acquires a named mutex (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">MTXCNBV11000ERCXSWOLZNBVRGH</code>) - exits if already running</li>
<li>Instantiates the victim profiler, C2 comms, and command dispatcher</li>
<li>Issues a single POST to the C2 with <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">payload: &quot;fetch&quot;</code>, handles any returned task</li>
<li>Exits - next execution is driven entirely by the 10-minute scheduled task trigger</li>
</ul>
<p></p>
<h3 class="font-bold leading-tight text-xl md:text-3xl relative"><span id="c2-communication" class="absolute -top-32"></span>C2 Communication</h3>
<p>The malware communicates with its C2 by issuing HTTP POST requests with the Content-Type set to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">application/x-www-form-urlencoded</code>. Each field value is independently AES-256-CBC encrypted with a random IV. The AES key is derived as the SHA-256 hash of the hardcoded communications passphrase (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">AnCnDai@4zDsxP!a3E</code>). The IV is prepended to the ciphertext, and the entire blob is base64-encoded; C2 responses follow the same format.</p>
<pre><code>encrypted_field_value = base64_encode(random_iv + AES-256-CBC_encrypt\
 (key: SHA-256(&#x27;AnCnDai@4zDsxP!a3E&#x27;), iv: random_iv, data: plaintext_field_value))</code></pre>
<p>Fields sent on every request:</p>
<div class="table-container"><table style="width:100%;table-layout:fixed;word-wrap:break-word"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Field</th><th style="text-align:left">Value</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">desktop</code></td><td style="text-align:left">machine name</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">username</code></td><td style="text-align:left">username</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">os</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Windows version</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">version</code></td><td style="text-align:left">bot version (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">1.1.6.</code>)</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">privileges</code></td><td style="text-align:left">user OR admin</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">cpu</code></td><td style="text-align:left">processor name from the registry</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">gpu</code></td><td style="text-align:left">GPU name(s) from registry</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">gpu_type</code></td><td style="text-align:left">yes (discrete) / no (integrated)</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">group</code></td><td style="text-align:left">group / campaign ID (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">03_26</code>)</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">client_path</code></td><td style="text-align:left">full path of running executable</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">local_ipv4</code></td><td style="text-align:left">external IP via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">ipify[.]org</code> / <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">icanhazip[.]com</code> / <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">ident[.]me</code></td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">auth_token</code></td><td style="text-align:left">authentication token (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">0326GJSECMHSHOEYHQMKDZ</code>)</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">timestamp</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Unix epoch (UTC)</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">payload</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Command string (“fetch”, “completed”)</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<p>A server response decrypts to either a task string, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">“NO TASKS”</code>, or <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">“REGISTERED/UPDATED”</code>. When the client requests a task through <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">payload: “fetch”</code>, if a task exists for the client, the C2 response decrypts to a <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">&lt;sep&gt;</code>-delimited task string: <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">task_id&lt;sep&gt;command&lt;sep&gt;argument&lt;sep&gt;RSA_sig</code>.</p>
<p></p>
<p>Prior to dispatch, each task undergoes RSA-SHA256 signature verification. The signed message is the concatenated string <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">task_id&lt;sep&gt;command&lt;sep&gt;argument</code>, and the signature is the base64-decoded <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">RSA_sig</code> field. A hardcoded RSA-2048 public key is used for verification.</p>
<p></p>
<p>Tasks failing verification are silently dropped. Without the operator&#x27;s RSA private key, third parties cannot issue commands to infected hosts even with full C2 access.</p>
<h3 class="font-bold leading-tight text-xl md:text-3xl relative"><span id="supported-commands" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Supported Commands</h3>
<p>3 commands are supported, described in the table below:</p>
<div class="table-container"><table style="width:100%;table-layout:fixed;word-wrap:break-word"><thead><tr><th style="text-align:left">Command</th><th style="text-align:left">Behavior</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">download_execute</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Downloads URL argument to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%\&lt;random&gt;.&lt;ext&gt;</code>. Execute: .exe (hidden), .bat/.cmd (cmd /c), .vbs (wscript.exe), other (ShellExecute).</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">update</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Downloads URL argument to staging location <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%TEMP%\tmp_updt236974520367.exe</code>. Runs BAT to: kill current PID, overwrite installed binary with staged download, delete staging file, and self-delete BAT.</td></tr><tr><td style="text-align:left"><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">uninstall</code></td><td style="text-align:left">Deletes scheduled task, removes <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">install.dat</code>, self-deletes via BAT, rmdir install dir, and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%APPDATA%\HostData\</code>.</td></tr></tbody></table></div>
<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="earlier-campaigns" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Earlier Campaigns</h2>
<p>Pivoting on the PureRAT mutex <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Aesthetics135</code>, we discovered an earlier wave of the operation that presented a different fake installer UI.</p>
<p></p>
<h3 class="font-bold leading-tight text-xl md:text-3xl relative"><span id="early-2025-build" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Early 2025 Build</h3>
<p>The sample <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">bb48a52bae2ee8b98ee1888b3e7d05539c85b24548dd4c6acc08fbe5f0d7631a</code> (first seen 2025-01-30) is a Themida and .NET Reactor-protected Windows Forms application that drops PureRAT v0.3.9.</p>
<p>It consists of 3 classes: <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Fooo1rm</code> (the ApplicationContext entry point), <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Form2</code> (the installer UI and the PureRAT dropper), and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Form3</code> (a fake registration lure). The code structure closely resembles the more recent campaigns.</p>
<p>On initialization, it immediately invokes a hidden PowerShell one-liner to add itself to Microsoft Defender exclusions before any UI appears: <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">powershell.exe -WindowStyle Hidden Add-MpPreference -ExclusionPath &#x27;&lt;self_path&gt;&#x27;; Add-MpPreference -ExclusionProcess &#x27;&lt;self_path&gt;&#x27;</code>. A timer with a 2,846 ms interval fires, instantiating and showing Form2.</p>
<p><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Form2</code> presents a progress bar dialog titled “Getting things ready” with a 12-step timer ticking every 1,000 ms, simulating a legitimate installation.</p>
<p></p>
<p>A second PowerShell exclusion command covers <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%LocalAppData%</code>, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%AppData%</code>, the drop directory <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%LocalAppData%\winbuf</code>, and process names including <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">winbuf.exe</code>, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wintrs.exe</code>, and <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">AddlnProcess.exe</code>. The PureRAT v0.3.9 payload is extracted from the assembly manifest resource and written to <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">%LocalAppData%\winbuf\winbuf.exe</code>. Persistence is established via <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">schtasks.exe</code>.</p>
<p>Extracted PureRAT config:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.hopto.org</code> (C2 #1)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.itemdb.com</code> (C2 #2)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.kozow.com</code> (C2 #3)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.ydns.eu</code> (C2 #4)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Aesthetics135</code> (mutex and C2 comms key)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">29-01-25</code> (build / campaign date)</li>
</ul>
<p><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Form3</code> serves purely as a social engineering mechanism to drive <a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cost_per_action">Cost Per Action</a> (CPA) offer completions through a content locker.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Content lockers are a monetization technique in which access to a resource is gated behind completing CPA (Cost Per Action) offers, such as filling out a survey or signing up for a service. The malware operator earns a commission each time a victim completes one of these offers.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>It presents a fake “Registration Required” dialog with a key entry field, a “Validate” button, and a hyperlink labeled “here” that opens <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://tinyurl[.]com/cmvt944y</code>. Key validation is entirely fake. Regardless of input, the handler introduces a hardcoded 2-second delay, then always returns “Invalid key. Please try again.”</p>
<p></p>
<p>The TinyURL shortlink <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">tinyurl[.]com/cmvt944y</code> redirects to the lure page at <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">rapidfilesdatabaze[.]top/files/z872d515ea17b4e6c3abca9752c706242/</code>.</p>
<p>The page used to host a minimal HTML document titled &quot;Registration Key is Ready&quot;, designed to trick the victim into interacting with the CPA content locker. It presents a download icon and a fake file link labeled <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Registration_Key.txt</code>, alongside a unique campaign tracking ID (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">z872d515ea17b4e6c3abca9752c706242</code>) displayed in the page body.</p>
<p>The content locker JavaScript (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">3193171.js</code>) is loaded from <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">d3nxbjuv18k2dn.cloudfront[.]net</code>, and clicking the <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Registration_Key.txt</code> link triggers the offer wall under the pretext of unlocking a license key.</p>
<p></p>
<p></p>
<h3 class="font-bold leading-tight text-xl md:text-3xl relative"><span id="late-2023-build" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Late 2023 Build</h3>
<p>An older sample - <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">6a01cc61f367d3bae34439f94ff3599fcccb66d05a8e000760626abb9886beac</code> (first seen 2023-11-09) presented a similar fake installer UI. This represents the earliest activity we attributed to this threat actor based on shared infrastructure and tooling.</p>
<p></p>
<p>This campaign build dropped PureRAT v0.3.8B, in which the in-memory PE loader component used a SmartAssembly-protected PureCrypter.</p>
<p>Extracted PureRAT config:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.hopto.org</code> (C2 #1)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.itemdb.com</code> (C2 #2)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.kozow.com</code> (C2 #3)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">wndlogon.ydns.eu</code> (C2 #4)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Aesthetics135</code> (mutex and C2 comms key)</li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">09.11.23</code> (build / campaign date)</li>
</ul>
<p>On the installation window, the “go here” hyperlink opens a short link <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://t[.]ly/MQXPm</code> that redirects to the lure page <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://softwaredlfast[.]top/files/n71fGbs2b7XceW3op71aQsrx41Rkeydl/</code>, which presents 2 outgoing fake download links:</p>
<ul>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://rapidfilesbaze[.]top/z78fGbs2b7XceWop21aQsrx41Rkeydsktp/</code></li>
<li><code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://rapidfilesbaze[.]top/z78fGbs2b7XceWop21aQsrx41Rkeymbl/</code></li>
</ul>
<p></p>
<p>Both links were offline at the time of analysis. However, historical data indicates that <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">rapidfilesbaze[.]top</code> has been used consistently for CPA-style offer lures.</p>
<p>A <a href="http://URLScan.io">URLScan.io</a> archived response for a related path (<code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">rapidfilesbaze[.]top/h74fGbs2b7XceWop71aQsrx41-Registration-Key-Mobile/</code>) confirms the site&#x27;s use as a lure landing page.</p>
<p></p>
<p>The downstream unlocker site at <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://unlockcontent[.]net/cl/i/me9mn2</code> remains active as of this writing.</p>
<p></p>
<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="github-profiles" class="absolute -top-32"></span>GitHub Profiles</h2>
<p>Beyond the C2 infrastructure, the threat actor abuses GitHub as a payload delivery CDN, hosting staged binaries across two identified accounts. This technique shifts the download-and-execute step away from operator-controlled infrastructure to a trusted platform, reducing detection friction. Both profiles were confirmed through decrypting C2 task traffic captured by VirusTotal sandboxes, which issued download-and-execute tasks pointing directly to raw GitHub content URLs. The operator routinely deletes individual binaries and entire repositories; the files documented below were captured via VirusTotal submissions or direct retrieval from GitHub prior to deletion.</p>
<p>The first profile, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://github[.]com/lebnabar198</code>, surfaced during analysis of Campaign 2. After decrypting the C2 traffic from the <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">windirautoupdates[.]top</code> server, we observed the PureRAT implant being instructed to fetch a payload from this account, specifically the custom XMRig loader <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">MnrsInstllr_240126.exe</code>. This establishes a direct operational link between the PureRAT C2 and this GitHub profile.</p>
<p>The second profile, <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">https://github[.]com/ugurlutaha6116</code>, was identified by decrypting traffic from a PureRAT loader (SHA-256: <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">e1e87d11079d33ec1a1c25629cbb747e56fe17071bde5fd8c982461b5baa80a4</code>), which used the same PBKDF2 key derivation structure with the comms key <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">Aesthetics152</code>. The decrypted task pointed to the hosted payload <code class="px-1.5 py-1 rounded not-prose bg-[var(--tw-prose-invert-pre-bg)] whitespace-break-spaces text-[85%] text-emerald-600">PM3107.exe</code>.</p>
<p>The hosted files map to the following payloads:</p>

<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="monero-wallet-analysis" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Monero Wallet Analysis</h2>
<p>During our analysis of the cryptominer payloads, we successfully extracted four active Monero (XMR) wallet addresses from the malware&#x27;s configuration. Because the threat actor is routing their compromised hosts through public mining pools, we can query the pool&#x27;s public dashboards using these wallet addresses. It provides information about the operational scale and profitability of the campaigns.</p>
<p></p>

<p>With a combined total of over 27.88 XMR (~ USD$ 9392) already successfully paid out to the attacker, it proves that low-and-slow cryptojacking operations can yield consistent financial returns over time.</p>
<h2 class="font-bold text-2xl md:text-4xl relative"><span id="agentic-payload-and-configuration-extraction-pipeline" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Agentic Payload and Configuration Extraction Pipeline</h2>
<p>In this research, we examined several hundred infection chains across the campaigns we described. For each chain, we have samples, mainly .NET, which are either loaders or final payloads layered with .NET Reactor obfuscation and often Themida packing.</p>
<p>The large number of these chains makes manual configuration and unpacking time-consuming and difficult to scale across all the chains we discovered. This is why, as part of this research, we used the Claude Opus 4.5 model to quickly vibecode a payload and configuration extraction pipeline. In this section, we provide details on the choices we made and the results we obtained with this method.</p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="triage" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Triage</h4>
<p>To optimize processing time, this phase focuses on extensively exploring infection chains using VirusTotal. We begin by obtaining a list of hashes from VirusTotal based on a specific pivot. For instance, using the README.txt content as a pivot to identify other ISOs.</p>
<p></p>
<p>Claude is instructed to use a Python script to perform a recursive download. This process involves gathering information about embedded binaries and dropped files associated with each file hash. Claude then uses its “intelligence” to identify the most subsequent link in the chain and continues its investigation until it reaches what it considers the final binary in that chain. After exploring all chains, Claude analyzes the patterns and creates chain types to group them. Finally, the results are compiled into a CSV file for subsequent analysis.</p>
<p>The data we obtained includes the starting hash from VirusTotal and the final hash, representing the last file Claude successfully tracked. This demonstrates that, with the right guidance, Claude can effectively track entire chains using only information from VirusTotal.</p>
<p></p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="download-and-extraction" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Download and Extraction</h4>
<p>Once the triage file was created, we downloaded the intermediate payloads and instructed Claude to start the automatic payload/configuration extraction process. To do this, we installed an OpenSSH server on a Windows virtual machine, then created a Claude skill containing instructions to connect to this machine and use the installed tools to perform the reverse engineering and extraction workflow.</p>
<p>The workflow is simple: Claude connects to the machine, uploads the sample, detects whether it is obfuscated or packed with Detect It Easy, and applies the appropriate deobfuscation tool until the sample is no longer obfuscated (Unlicense, .NET Reactor Slayer). It then runs the developed extraction scripts to identify what the sample is and determine the next step: either continue extraction with the child payload if the parent is a loader, or store the configuration information for the final report.</p>
<p></p>
<p>If all the extraction scripts fail, Claude must enter Research Mode. This mode is the most enjoyable part of the skill because it gives Claude a workflow to either automatically develop a new extraction script or identify why the existing script doesn&#x27;t work with the variant. Claude’s Research Mode consists of using the <a href="https://github.com/dnSpyEx">dnSpyEx</a> tool installed on the machine to compile the sample&#x27;s C# code, perform a complete code analysis, identify how to extract the payload or configuration, then develop a script with this knowledge to work directly with the raw binaries to be more efficient and finally store the knowledge for the next time it has to work on the same malware family.</p>
<p></p>
<h4 class="font-bold leading-tight text-lg md:text-2xl relative"><span id="results" class="absolute -top-32"></span>Results</h4>
<p>Using the Claude Opus 4.5 model, the results were really good. Not only did Claude succeed in handling the obfuscation layers, but it also completely researched and developed, on its own, the methods and scripts (based on the CIL of .NET binaries) to extract the final payloads and their configurations without having encountered them before.</p>
<p>It also demonstrated robust failure handling without requiring additional instruction. For example, when it encountered samples that could not be fully deobfuscated due to issues with Reactor Slayer, which made static extraction too difficult, it stopped processing, documented the problem, and proceeded to the next sample.</p>
<p></p>
<p>Of course, it is not without drawbacks:</p>
<ul>
<li>Once its context started to fill up too much, it often diverged onto useless paths and required either micro-management or a reset, hence the usefulness of having a skill with reusable instructions and a knowledge base on the work already done.</li>
<li>It takes a long time, every action requires it to “think”, however, it’s automatic and it&#x27;s definitely time recovered you can use to do something else.</li>
<li>Its token consumption is particularly greedy, especially once you figure out it’s doing a lot of inefficient things.</li>
</ul>
